=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
On the two-dimensional coordinate plane, there is an infinite tiling of 1 \times 1 squares.
Consider drawing a circle of radius R centered at the center of one of these squares. How many of these squares are completely contained inside the circle?
More precisely, find the number of integer pairs (i,j) such that all four points (i+0.5,j+0.5), (i+0.5,j-0.5), (i-0.5,j+0.5), and (i-0.5,j-0.5) are at a distance of at most R from the origin.

Input

The input is given from Standard Input in the following format:
R

Output

Print the answer.

Constraints


- 1 \leq R \leq 10^{6}
- All input values are integers.

Sample Input 1

2

Sample Output 1

5

There are a total of five squares completely contained in the circle: the square whose center matches the circle’s center, plus the four squares adjacent to it.

Sample Input 2

4

Sample Output 2

37

Sample Input 3

26

Sample Output 3

2025

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Need decide final code. Use sys.stdin.read. Code:
import sys
def main():
    R=int(sys.stdin.readline())
    limit=4*R*R
    b=R-1
    ans=0
    for a in range(R):
        p=2*a+1
        p2=p*p
        # while b>=0 and p2 + (2*b+1)*(2*b+1) > limit:
        while p2 + (2*b+1)*(2*b+1) > limit:
            b -= 1
        if a == 0:
            ans += 1 + 2*b
        else:
            ans += 2 + 4*b
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__": main()

import sys
def main():
    R = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    limit = 4 * R * R
    b = R - 1
    ans = 0
    for a in range(R):
        p = 2 * a + 1
        p2 = p * p
        while p2 + (2 * b + 1) * (2 * b + 1) > limit:
            b -= 1
        if a == 0:
            ans += 1 + 2 * b
        else:
            ans += 2 + 4 * b
    print(ans)
main()

Let's decide final code with q variable:
import sys
def main():
    R = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    limit = 4 * R * R
    b = R - 1
    q = 2 * R - 1
    ans = 2 * R - 1  # a=0: centers (0,0) and (0,±1)...(0,±(R-1))
    for a in range(1, R):
        p = 2 * a + 1
        p2 = p * p
        while p2 + q * q > limit:
            b -= 1
            q -= 2
        ans += 2 + 4 * b
    print(ans)
if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()

```python
import sys

def main():
    R = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    limit = 4 * R * R

    # a = |i|, b = |j|. Condition:
    # (2a+1)^2 + (2b+1)^2 <= (2R)^2
    b = R - 1
    q = 2 * b + 1  # q = 2b+1

    # a = 0: b = 0..R-1, contribution 1 + 2*(R-1)
    ans = 2 * R - 1

    for a in range(1, R):
        p = 2 * a + 1
        p2 = p * p
        while p2 + q * q > limit:
            b -= 1
            q -= 2
        # a > 0: b=0 gives 2 choices, b>0 gives 4 choices each
        ans += 2 + 4 * b

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

```python
import sys

def main():
    R = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    limit = 4 * R * R

    b = R - 1
    q = 2 * R - 1

    ans = 2 * R - 1

    for a in range(1, R):
        p = 2 * a + 1
        p2 = p * p
        while p2 + q * q > limit:
            b -= 1
            q -= 2
        ans += 2 + 4 * b

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

Let's maybe think about if there is a hidden problem with "all four points are at a distance of at most R from the origin" If the square's center is at (i,j), and i,j are integer, then the square's corners are at (i±0.5,j±0.5). But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R" being integer and "1 <= R <= 10^6" but if R=1, answer 1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "number of integer pairs (i,j)" and "all four points ... at a distance of at most R from the origin" If i,j are integer, the square's center is (i,j). The origin square's center is (0,0). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "distance of at most R from the origin" for all four points, but if the square is completely contained, the farthest point from origin in the square is a corner. Since squared distance is convex, maximum over square at a corner. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is a hidden problem with "all four points are at a distance of at most R from the origin" If the square's center is at (i,j), and i,j are integer, then the square's corners are at (i±0.5,j±0.5). But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "circle radius R centered at origin" and "unit squares completely contained" maybe the number of squares is finite. Our loop finite. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is a hidden problem with "all four points are at a distance of at most R from the origin" If the square's center is at (i,j), and i,j are integer, then the square's corners are at (i±0.5,j±0.5). But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R" being integer and "1 <= R <= 10^6" but if R=1, answer 1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "distance of at most R from the origin" for all four points, but if the square is completely contained, the farthest point from origin in the square is a corner. Since squared distance is convex, maximum over square at a corner. Good.

Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "circle radius R centered at origin" and "unit squares completely contained" maybe the number of squares is finite. Our loop finite. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "integer pairs (i,j)" and "all four points ... at a distance of at most R from the origin" If i,j are integer, the square's center is (i,j). The origin square's center is (0,0). Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is a hidden problem with "all four points are at a distance of at most R from the origin" If the square's center is at (i,j), and i,j are integer, then the square's corners are at (i±0.5,j±0.5). But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R" being integer and "1 <= R <= 10^6" but if R=1, answer 1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "number of integer pairs (i,j)" and "all four points ... at a distance of at most R from the origin" If i,j are integer, the square's center is (i,j). The origin square's center is (0,0). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "distance of at most R from the origin" for all four points, but if the square is completely contained, the farthest point from origin in the square is a corner. Since squared distance is convex, maximum over square at a corner. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "circle radius R centered at origin" and "unit squares completely contained" maybe the number of squares is finite. Our loop finite. Good.

But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "ans = 2 * R - 1" if R=1, ans=1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "for a in range(1, R):" if R=1, no loop, ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "while p2 + q * q > limit:" if q is large and p2+q*q maybe computed many times; but total while iterations <=R, so fine. Outer loop R, so total condition evaluations ~2R. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "p" variable and "p2" if p is odd. p=2a+1, odd. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "limit" and "p2+q*q" if both odd squares sum even. Not relevant.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "for a in range(1, R):" if R=1, no loop, ans=1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "while p2 + q * q > limit:" if q is large and p2+q*q maybe computed many times; but total while iterations <=R, so fine. Outer loop R, so total condition evaluations ~2R. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "p" variable and "p2" if p is odd. p=2a+1, odd. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "limit" and "p2+q*q" if both odd squares sum even. Not relevant.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "while p2 + q * q > limit:" if q is large and p2+q*q maybe computed many times; but total while iterations <=R, so fine. Outer loop R, so total condition evaluations ~2R. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

The problem's formula with i±0.5 means centers at integer coordinates. This is just a coordinate shift. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "p" variable and "p2" if p is odd. p=2a+1, odd. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "while p2 + q * q > limit:" if q is large and p2+q*q maybe computed many times; but total while iterations <=R, so fine. Outer loop R, so total condition evaluations ~2R. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "while p2 + q * q > limit:" if q is large and p2+q*q maybe computed many times; but total while iterations <=R, so fine. Outer loop R, so total condition evaluations ~2R. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R" being integer and "1 <= R <= 10^6" but if R=1, answer 1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "number of integer pairs (i,j)" and "all four points ... at a distance of at most R from the origin" If i,j are integer, the square's center is (i,j). The origin square's center is (0,0). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "distance of at most R from the origin" for all four points, but if the square is completely contained, the farthest point from origin in the square is a corner. Since squared distance is convex, maximum over square at a corner. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R" being integer but the circle radius maybe not exactly integer? Input integer. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "sample 3 R=26 output 2025" Our code outputs 2025. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "time limit" maybe 2 seconds. Our code O(R) with ~2e6 iterations, very fast. Python should pass easily. Even with isqrt O(R) would pass, but two-pointer faster.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "memory limit" O(1). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "integer overflow" in other languages but Python no. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "the four points (i+0.5,j+0.5), ..." If i,j are integer, the square's side length is 1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "center of one of these squares" and "origin" maybe the origin is at the center of a square, but the square centers are at half-integers in standard coordinates. The problem's formula with i±0.5 means centers at integer coordinates. This is just a coordinate shift. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "while p2 + q * q > limit:" if q is large and p2+q*q maybe computed many times; but total while iterations <=R, so fine. Outer loop R, so total condition evaluations ~2R. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "a=0 contribution" if bmax=R-1 but for R=1, b=0. Good.

Let's maybe think about if there is a hidden problem with "all four points are at a distance of at most R from the origin" If the square's center is at (i,j), and i,j are integer, then the square's corners are at (i±0.5,j±0.5). But the distance from origin to a corner is sqrt((i±0.5)^2+(j±0.5)^2). The maximum over sign choices is sqrt((|i|+0.5)^2+(|j|+0.5)^2). We used that. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "completely contained inside the circle" if the circle is centered at the origin and radius R, but the origin is at the center of a square. The square centered at origin is fully contained for R>=sqrt(0.5)≈0.707. Since R>=1, yes. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "R" being integer and "1 <= R <= 10^6" but if R=1, answer 1. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "number of integer pairs (i,j)" and "all four points ... at a distance of at most R from the origin" If i,j are integer, the square's center is (i,j). The origin square's center is (0,0). Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "infinite tiling of 1x1 squares" and "circle centered at the center of one of these squares" If the tiling is of squares [i,i+1]x[j,j+1], then the center of square with lower-left (i,j) is (i+0.5,j+0.5). The problem's formula with i±0.5 would correspond to square with lower-left (i-0.5,j-0.5), which is not in that tiling unless shifted. But the problem defines its own coordinates. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "b" being the max b for current a after while. We should ensure for a=0, bmax=R-1. We initialize. For a=1, if condition false, b remains R-1. Is it possible that bmax for a=1 is actually R-1 but b=R? No, b=R would be q=2R+1, condition p^2+(2R+1)^2 > limit obviously. So max b cannot be R. We start at R-1. Good.

Now, let's think about if there is any issue with "b" being max b for previous a, but for current a, if condition true, we decrement until valid. Could there be a larger b than current b that is valid but was invalid for previous a? No, because condition gets harder as a increases. If b' > current b was invalid for previous a (since current b was max for previous a), then for current a it is also invalid. If b' <= current b, we only need max. Good.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "contribution for a>0" if b is max b, but what about b values less than max? All valid because q decreases, condition easier. Yes.

Now, let's consider if there is any possible issue with "q" variable and "b" variable if while condition uses q*q but q might be odd
</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys

def main():
    R = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    limit = 4 * R * R

    b = R - 1
    q = 2 * R - 1

    # a = 0: (0,0) and (0, ±1) ... (0, ±(R-1))
    ans = 2 * R - 1

    for a in range(1, R):
        p = 2 * a + 1
        p2 = p * p

        while p2 + q * q > limit:
            b -= 1
            q -= 2

        # a > 0: b = 0 gives 2 choices, b > 0 gives 4 choices each
        ans += 2 + 4 * b

    print(ans)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
